On the infinite time horizon approximation for Lévy-driven McKean-Vlasov SDEs with non-globally Lipschitz continuous and super-linearly growth drift and diffusion coefficients
Ngoc Khue Tran, Trung-Thuy Kieu, Duc-Trong Luong, Hoang-Long Ngo

Abstract
This paper studies the numerical approximation for McKean-Vlasov stochastic differential equations driven by Lévy processes. We propose a tamed-adaptive Euler-Maruyama scheme and consider its strong convergence in both finite and infinite time horizons when applying for some classes of Lévy-driven McKean-Vlasov stochastic differential equations with non-globally Lipschitz continuous and super-linearly growth drift and diffusion coefficients.



Keywords
Lévy process
\cdot
McKean-Vlasov
\cdot
Stochastic differential equation
\cdot
Super-linearly growth coefficient
\cdot
Tamed-adaptive Euler Maruyama

Mathematics Subject Classification:
60H35, 60H10

1
Introduction

On a complete probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
, we consider the
d
-dimensional process
X=(X_{t})_{t\geq 0}
solution to the following McKean-Vlasov stochastic differential equation (SDE) with jumps

dX_{t}=b(X_{t},\mathcal{L}_{X_{t}})dt+\sigma(X_{t},\mathcal{L}_{X_{t}})dW_{t}+c\left(X_{t-},\mathcal{L}_{X_{t-}}\right)dZ_{t},

(1)

for
t\geq 0
, where
X_{0}=x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a fixed initial value,
\mathcal{L}_{X_{t}}
denotes the marginal law of the process
X
at time
t
,
W=(W_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is a
d
-dimensional standard Brownian motion and
Z=(Z_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is a
d
-dimensional centered pure jump Lévy process whose Lévy measure
\nu
satisfies
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1\wedge|z|^{2})\nu(dz)<+\infty
. Two processes
W
and
Z
are supposed to be independent. The natural filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is generated by
W
and
Z
. The Lévy-Itô decomposition of
Z
is given by

Z_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}z(N(ds,dz)-\nu(dz)ds),

for any
t\geq 0
, where
\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}:=\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
, and
N(dt,dz)
is a Poisson random measure on the measurable space
(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d},\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}))
that is associated with the jumps of the Lévy process
Z
with intensity measure
\nu(dz)dt
. That is,

N(dt,dz):=\sum_{0\leq s\leq t}{\bf 1}_{\{\Delta Z_{s}\neq 0\}}\delta_{(s,\Delta Z_{s})}(ds,dz).

Here, the jump size of
Z
at instant
s
is defined as
\Delta Z_{s}:=Z_{s}-Z_{s-}:=Z_{s}-\lim_{u\uparrow s}Z_{u}
for any
s>0
,
\Delta Z_{0}:=0
,
\delta_{(s,z)}
denotes the Dirac measure at the point
(s,z)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}
, and
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d})
denotes the Borel
\sigma
-algebra on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}
. The compensated Poisson random measure associated with
N(dt,dz)
is denoted by
\widetilde{N}(dt,dz):=N(dt,dz)-\nu(dz)dt
.

We denote by
\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
the space of all probability measures defined on a measurable space
(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, where
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
denotes the Borel
\sigma
-field over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and by

\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):=\left\{\mu\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\mu(dx)<\infty\right\}

the subset of probability measures with the finite second moment. As metric on the space
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we use the
\mathcal{L}_{2}
-Wasserstein distance. That is, for
\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the
\mathcal{L}_{2}
-Wasserstein distance between
\mu
and
\nu
is defined as

\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\nu):=\inf_{\pi\in\mathcal{C}(\mu,\nu)}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|x-y\right|^{2}\pi(dx,dy)\right)^{1/2},

where
\mathcal{C}(\mu,\nu)
denotes all the couplings of
\mu
and
\nu
. That is,
\pi\in\mathcal{C}(\mu,\nu)
if and only if
\pi(\cdot,\mathbb{R}^{d})=\mu(\cdot)
and
\pi(\mathbb{R}^{d},\cdot)=\nu(\cdot)
.

The coefficients
b=(b_{i})_{1\leq i\leq d}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\sigma=(\sigma_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq d}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}^{d}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
c=(c_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq d}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}^{d}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{d}
are measurable functions. The integral equation (
1
) now writes as

X_{t}=x_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}b(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}})ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}})dW_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}c\left(X_{s-},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s-}}\right)z\widetilde{N}(ds,dz),

for any
t\geq 0
.

The McKean-Vlasov process was first studied by McKean in
[
26
]
as a model for the Vlasov equation describing the time evolution of the distribution function of a plasma consisting of charged particles with long-range interaction. The process can be obtained as a limit of a mean-field system of interacting particles as the number of particles tends to infinity. The very first studies on the numerical approximation for McKean-Vlasov SDEs are the works of Ogawa
[
28
]
, Kohatsu-Higa and Ogawa
[
18
]
and Bossy and Talay
[
3
]
, where the authors considered the weak approximation of McKean-Vlasov SDEs with regular coefficients. However, the numerical approximation for McKean-Vlasov SDEs has only become active in the last decade.

Let
X^{(n)}_{T}
be an approximation of
X_{T}
which depends on the values of
W
and
Z
at fixed equidistance times
t_{k}=\frac{kT}{n},k=0,1,\ldots,n
. Then under some regularity conditions on the coefficients
b,\sigma,
and
c
, one may prove that

\|X^{(n)}_{T}-X_{T}\|_{L^{p}}:=\Big(\mathbb{E}[|X^{(n)}_{T}-X_{T}|^{p}]\Big)^{1/p}\leq\frac{C(T)}{n^{\zeta_{0}}},

(2)

for some positive constants
p
and
{\zeta_{0}}
. In case the estimate (
2
) holds, we say that the
X^{(n)}_{T}
converges at the rate of order
{\zeta_{0}}
in
L^{p}
-norm.

It is now well-known that for SDEs with super-linear growth coefficients, the Euler-Maruyama scheme may not converge in the
L^{p}
-norm (see
[
13
]
). Therefore, the numerical approximation for SDEs with super-linear growth coefficients has attracted lots of attention recently. New approximation schemes have been introduced to solve the problem, such as the tamed Euler-Maruyama scheme (
[
14
,
31
,
32
,
21
]
), the truncated Euler-Maruyama scheme (
[
24
]
), the implicit Euler-Maruyama scheme (
[
25
]
), the adaptive Euler-Maruyama scheme (
[
10
]
). In
[
7
,
19
,
20
,
23
]
the tamed Euler-Maruyama scheme has been developed to approximate McKean-Vlasov SDEs with super-linear growth coefficients. In
[
29
]
, the authors introduced several adaptive Euler-Maruyama and Milstein schemes and studied their strong rate of convergence for McKean-Vlasov SDEs with super-linear drift.
In
[
15
]
, the authors introduced a multilevel Picard approximation, which has a low computational cost, for McKean-Vlasov SDEs with additive noise. In
[
6
,
7
]
, the authors introduced the implicit Euler-Maruyama scheme and studied its convergence in
L^{p}
-norm for McKean-Vlasov SDEs with drifts of super-linear growth.

The McKean-Vlasov SDEs with jumps were studied by many authors with applications in many domains (see
[
12
,
8
,
9
,
1
,
11
]
and the reference therein). In
[
27
]
, the authors considered McKean-Vlasov SDEs driven by infinite activity Lévy processes with super-linear growth coefficients. They proved the existence and uniqueness of the solution and proposed a tamed Euler-Maruyama approximation for the associated interacting particle system and proved that the rate of its convergence in
L^{p}
-norm is arbitrarily close to 0.5.

In some applications, it is necessary to approximate
X_{T}
for
T
large, e.g., to approximate the invariant distribution of process
X
(see Section 3 in
[
10
]
). However, since the proof of convergence of many schemes often involves Gronwall’s inequality, the quantity
C(T)
may grow exponentially fast to
T
. Recently, there have been some attempts to introduce numerical schemes where
C(T)
does not depend on
T
. The paper
[
10
]
proposed an adaptive Euler-Maruyama scheme for SDEs where each stepsize is adjusted to the size of the current values of
X
, and showed its strong convergence in the interval
[0,\infty)
when applying for SDEs whose coefficients
b
and
\sigma
satisfy the contractive Lipschitz condition (Assumption 9 in
[
10
]
),
b
is polynomial growth Lipschitz continuous, and
\sigma
is bounded and globally Lipschitz continuous. The paper
[
16
]
introduced a tamed-adaptive Euler-Maruyama scheme and considered its rate of convergence in
L^{1}
-norm when applying for one-dimensional SDEs whose diffusion coefficient
\sigma
is of super-linearly growth. In
[
17
]
, the result of
[
16
]
was generalized for one-dimensional SDEs with jumps.

This paper aims to generalize the result of the papers
[
10
,
16
,
17
]
for multi-dimensional McKean-Vlasov SDEs with jumps. In particular, we propose a tamed-adaptive Euler-Maruyama approximation scheme for the Lévy-driven SDEs (
1
) where
\sigma
and
b
are non-globally Lipschitz continuous and of super-linearly growth, and
c
is Lipschitz continuous. We will study the strong convergence of the scheme in both finite and infinite time intervals. Note that in
[
29
]
the authors only considered the strong convergence of their adaptive scheme in a fixed time interval. In
[
27
]
, the tamed Euler-Maruyama scheme is also proven to converge in a fixed time interval when applying for McKean-Vlasov SDEs with jumps. To the best of our knowledge, our tamed-adaptive Euler-Maruyama scheme is the first approximation method for Lévy driven McKean-Vlasov SDEs that could be shown to converge in an infinite time horizon.

We denote the vector Euclidean norm of
v
by
|v|:=(|v_{1}|^{2}+|v_{2}|^{2}+\ldots+|v_{d}|^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
, the inner product of vectors
v
and
w
by
\langle v,w\rangle:=v_{1}w_{1}+v_{2}w_{2}+\ldots+v_{d}w_{d}
for any
v,w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the Frobenius matrix norm by
|A|:=\sqrt{\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}A^{2}_{ij}}
for all
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, the integer part of
a
by
[a]
, and the transpose of a vector or matrix
A
by
A^{\mathsf{T}}
. Moreover, the binomial coefficients are denoted by
{n\choose k}:=\frac{n!}{k!(n-k)!}
. In all that follows, we denote by
C
positive constants whose value may change from one line to the next.

The rest of this paper is structured as follows. In Section
2
, we introduce assumptions on the coefficients of equation (
1
) and show some moment estimates under these assumptions. In Section
3
, we prove the propagation of chaos. In Section
4
, we introduce the tamed-adaptive Euler-Maruyama scheme and prove that it is well-defined and converges in
L^{2}
-norm. Section
5
presents a numerical study for the tamed-adaptive Euler-Maruyama scheme.

2
Model assumptions and moment estimates

Assume that the drift, diffusion and jump coefficients
b,\sigma,c
and the Lévy measure
\nu
of equation (
1
) satisfy the following conditions:

A1.

There exists a positive constant
L
such that

\displaystyle 2\left\langle x,b(x,\mu)\right\rangle+\left|\sigma(x,\mu)\right|^{2}+|c(x,\mu)|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)\leq L\left(1+|x|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})\right),

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

A2.

There exist constants
\kappa_{1}>0,\kappa_{2}>0
,
L_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
and
L_{2}\geq 0
such that

\displaystyle 2\left\langle x-\overline{x},b(x,\mu)-b(\overline{x},\overline{\mu})\right\rangle+\kappa_{1}\left|\sigma(x,\mu)-\sigma(\overline{x},\overline{\mu})\right|^{2}+\kappa_{2}|c(x,\mu)-c(\overline{x},\overline{\mu})|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)

\displaystyle\leq L_{1}|x-\overline{x}|^{2}+L_{2}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\overline{\mu}),

for any
x,\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu,\overline{\mu}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

A3.

b(x,\mu)
is a continuous function of
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

A4.

There exist constants
L>0
and
\ell\geq 0
such that

\displaystyle\left|b(x,\mu)-b(\overline{x},\overline{\mu})\right|\leq L\left(1+|x|^{\ell}+|\overline{x}|^{\ell}\right)\left(|x-\overline{x}|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\overline{\mu})\right),

for any
x,\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu,\overline{\mu}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

A5.

There exists an even integer
p_{0}\in[2,+\infty)
such that
\int_{|z|>1}|z|^{p_{0}}\nu(dz)<\infty
and
\int_{0<|z|\leq 1}|z|\nu(dz)<\infty
.

A6.

There exists a positive constant
L_{3}
such that

|c(x,\mu)|\leq L_{3}\left(1+|x|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})\right),

for any
\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

A7.

For the even integer
p_{0}\in[2,+\infty)
given in
A5
and the positive constant
L_{3}
given in
A6
, there exist constants
\gamma_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\gamma_{2}\geq 0
and
\eta\geq 0
such that

\displaystyle\langle x,b(x,\mu)\rangle+\dfrac{p_{0}-1}{2}|\sigma(x,\mu)|^{2}+\dfrac{1}{2L_{3}}|c(x,\mu)|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|\left(\left(1+L_{3}|z|\right)^{p_{0}-1}-1\right)\nu(dz)\leq\gamma_{1}|x|^{2}+\gamma_{2}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})+\eta,

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Remark 2.1
.

(i) It follows from Condition
A4
that

\displaystyle\left|b(x,\mu)\right|\leq\left|b(0,\delta_{0})\right|+L\left(1+|x|^{\ell}\right)\left(|x|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})\right),

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

(ii) Assume that Condition
A2
holds for
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}=1+\varepsilon
,
L_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
,
L_{2}\geq 0
with a constant
\varepsilon>0
. This, combined with Condition
A4
and Cauchy’s inequality, implies that

\displaystyle\left(1+\varepsilon\right)\left|\sigma(x,\mu)-\sigma(\overline{x},\overline{\mu})\right|^{2}+\left(1+\varepsilon\right)|c(x,\mu)-c(\overline{x},\overline{\mu})|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)

\displaystyle\leq 2|x-\overline{x}||b(x,\mu)-b(\overline{x},\overline{\mu})|+L_{1}|x-\overline{x}|^{2}+L_{2}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\overline{\mu})

\displaystyle\leq L\left(1+|x|^{\ell}+|\overline{x}|^{\ell}\right)\left(2|x-\overline{x}|^{2}+2|x-\overline{x}|\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\overline{\mu})\right)+L_{1}|x-\overline{x}|^{2}+L_{2}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\overline{\mu})

\displaystyle\leq L\left(1+|x|^{\ell}+|\overline{x}|^{\ell}\right)\left(2|x-\overline{x}|^{2}+|x-\overline{x}|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\overline{\mu})\right)+L_{1}|x-\overline{x}|^{2}+L_{2}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\overline{\mu})

\displaystyle\leq L\widetilde{L}\left(1+|x|^{\ell}+|\overline{x}|^{\ell}\right)\left(|x-\overline{x}|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\overline{\mu})\right),

with
\widetilde{L}=\max\{3,L_{1},L_{2}\}
for any
x,\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu,\overline{\mu}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. This yields to

\displaystyle\left|\sigma(x,\mu)-\sigma(\overline{x},\overline{\mu})\right|^{2}+|c(x,\mu)-c(\overline{x},\overline{\mu})|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)\leq\dfrac{L\widetilde{L}}{1+\varepsilon}\left(1+|x|^{\ell}+|\overline{x}|^{\ell}\right)\left(|x-\overline{x}|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\overline{\mu})\right),

for any
x,\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu,\overline{\mu}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

(iii) From (ii), we have that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\displaystyle\left|\sigma(x,\mu)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left|\sigma(x,\mu)-\sigma(0,\delta_{0})\right|^{2}+2\left|\sigma(0,\delta_{0})\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\dfrac{L\widetilde{L}}{1+\varepsilon}\left(1+|x|^{\ell}\right)\left(|x|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})\right)+2\left|\sigma(0,\delta_{0})\right|^{2},

and similarly,

\displaystyle|c(x,\mu)|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)\leq 2\dfrac{L\widetilde{L}}{1+\varepsilon}\left(1+|x|^{\ell}\right)\left(|x|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})\right)+2\left|c(0,\delta_{0})\right|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{2}\nu(dz).

Remark 2.2
.

Observe that Condition
A7
yields to

\displaystyle\langle x,b(x,\mu)\rangle+\dfrac{p-1}{2}|\sigma(x,\mu)|^{2}+\dfrac{1}{2L_{3}}|c(x,\mu)|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|\left(\left(1+L_{3}|z|\right)^{p-1}-1\right)\nu(dz)\leq\gamma_{1}|x|^{2}+\gamma_{2}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})+\eta,

for any
p\in[2,p_{0}]
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

We first recall a result on the existence and uniqueness of the strong solution of McKean-Vlasov SDE with jumps (
1
) which is taken from
[
27
]
.

Proposition 2.3
.

(
[
27
, Theorem 2.1]
)
Assume Conditions
A1
,
A3
and that Condition
A2
holds for
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}=1,L_{1}=L_{2}>0
. Then, there exists a unique càdlàg process
X=(X_{t})_{t\geq 0}
taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfying the McKean-Vlasov SDE with jumps (
1
) such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{t}|^{2}\right]\leq K,

where
T>0
is a fixed constant and
K:=K(|x_{0}|^{2},d,L,L_{1},T)
is a positive constant.

We now recall the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality for the compensated Poisson stochastic integral which will be useful in the sequel.

Lemma 2.4
.

(
[
2
, Theorem 4.4.23]
and
[
33
, Proposition 2.2]
) Let
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d})
be the Borel
\sigma
-algebra of
\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}
and
\mathcal{P}
be the progressive
\sigma
-algebra on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\Omega
. Let
g
be a
\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d})
-measurable function satisfying that
\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|g(s,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)ds<\infty

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
T\geq 0
. Then for any
p\geq 2
, there exists a positive constant
C_{p}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}g(s,z)\widetilde{N}(ds,dz)\right|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|g(s,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|g(s,z)|^{p}\nu(dz)ds\right]\right).

Furthermore, for any
1\leq p<2
, there exists a positive constant
C_{p}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}g(s,z)\widetilde{N}(ds,dz)\right|^{p}\right]\leq C_{p}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|g(s,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)ds\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\right].

We next show the following moment estimates of the exact solution
X=(X_{t})_{t\geq 0}
.

Proposition 2.5
.

Let
X=(X_{t})_{t\geq 0}
be a solution to equation (
1
). Assume Conditions
A6, A7
, and that
\sigma
is bounded on
C\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for every compact subset
C
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
A5
holds for
q=2p_{0}
.
Then for any
p\in[2,p_{0}]
, there exists a positive constant
C_{p}
such that for any
t\geq 0

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{t}|^{p}\right]\leq\begin{cases}C_{p}(1+e^{\gamma pt})&\text{ if \;\;}\gamma\not=0,\\
C_{p}(1+t)^{p/2}&\text{ if \;\;}\gamma=0,p=2\text{\; or \;}\gamma=0,\gamma_{2}>0,p\in(2,p_{0}],\\
C_{p}(1+t)^{p}&\text{ if \;\;}\gamma=0,\gamma_{2}=0,p\in(2,p_{0}],\end{cases}\end{split}

(3)

where
\gamma=\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}
.

Note that if
\gamma<0
, we have that
\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{t}|^{p}\right]\leq 2C_{p}
.

Proof.

Step 1:
We first show that for any even natural number
p\in[2,p_{0}]
and
T>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|X_{t}\right|^{p}\right]\leq C(T,p).

(4)

Note that (
4
) holds for
p=2
due to Proposition
2.3
. Next, we assume that
(
4
) is valid for any even natural number
q\in[2,p-2]
. That is,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|X_{t}\right|^{q}\right]\leq C(T,q).

(5)

Now, for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and even natural number
p\in[2,p_{0}]
, applying Itô’s formula to
e^{-\lambda t}|X_{t}|^{p}
, we obtain that for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda t}|X_{t}|^{p}=|x_{0}|^{p}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda s}\bigg[-\lambda\left|X_{s}\right|^{p}+p\left|X_{s}\right|^{p-2}\left\langle X_{s},b\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)\right\rangle+\dfrac{p}{2}\left|X_{s}\right|^{p-2}\left|\sigma\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\dfrac{p(p-2)}{2}\left|X_{s}\right|^{p-4}\left|X_{s}^{\mathsf{T}}\sigma\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)\right|^{2}\bigg]ds+p\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda s}\left|X_{s}\right|^{p-2}\left\langle X_{s},\sigma\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)dW_{s}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}e^{-\lambda s}\left(\left|X_{s-}+c\left(X_{s-},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s-}}\right)z\right|^{p}-\left|X_{s-}\right|^{p}\right)\widetilde{N}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}e^{-\lambda s}\left(\left|X_{s}+c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z\right|^{p}-\left|X_{s}\right|^{p}-p\left|X_{s}\right|^{p-2}\left\langle X_{s},c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z\right\rangle\right)\nu(dz)ds.

(6)

In order to treat the last integral in (
6
), it suffices to use the binomial theorem to get that, for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left|X_{s}+c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z\right|^{p}=\left(\left|X_{s}+c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z\right|^{2}\right)^{p/2}

\displaystyle=\left(|X_{s}|^{2}+|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z|^{2}+2\left\langle X_{s},c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z\right\rangle\right)^{p/2}

\displaystyle=|X_{s}|^{p}+\dfrac{p}{2}|X_{s}|^{p-2}\left(|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z|^{2}+2\langle X_{s},c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=2}^{p/2}{p/2\choose i}|X_{s}|^{p-2i}\left(|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z|^{2}+2\left\langle X_{s},c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z\right\rangle\right)^{i}

\displaystyle=|X_{s}|^{p}+p|X_{s}|^{p-2}\left\langle X_{s},c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z\right\rangle+\dfrac{p}{2}|X_{s}|^{p-2}\left|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=2}^{p/2}{p/2\choose i}|X_{s}|^{p-2i}\left(|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z|^{2}+2\left\langle X_{s},c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z\right\rangle\right)^{i}.

(7)

Next, by using the binomial theorem repeatedly, Condition
A6
, the equality
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}\left(\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}},\delta_{0}\right)=\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]
, and the inequality
y|x|^{p-3}\leq\frac{1}{2}(|x|^{p-2}+y^{2}|x|^{p-4})
valid for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},y>0
,
\sum_{j=0}^{i}{i\choose j}a^{j}=(1+a)^{i}
and
\sum_{j=0}^{i}{i\choose j}ja^{j}=ia(1+a)^{i-1}
valid for any
a\in\mathbb{R}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle|X_{s}|^{p-2i}\left(|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z|^{2}+2\left\langle X_{s},c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z\right\rangle\right)^{i}=|X_{s}|^{p-2i}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{i\choose j}|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z|^{2i-2j}2^{j}\left(\langle X_{s},c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z\rangle\right)^{j}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{i}{i\choose j}2^{j}|X_{s}|^{p-2i+j}|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2i-j}|z|^{2i-j}

\displaystyle=|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{i\choose j}2^{j}|X_{s}|^{p-2i+j}|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2i-j-2}|z|^{2i-j}

\displaystyle\leq|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{i\choose j}2^{j}|X_{s}|^{p-2i+j}|z|^{2i-j}L_{3}^{2i-j-2}\left(1+|X_{s}|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}},\delta_{0})\right)^{2i-j-2}

\displaystyle=|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{i\choose j}2^{j}|X_{s}|^{p-2i+j}|z|^{2i-j}L_{3}^{2i-j-2}\left(1+|X_{s}|+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)^{2i-j-2}

\displaystyle=|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{i\choose j}2^{j}|X_{s}|^{p-2i+j}|z|^{2i-j}L_{3}^{2i-j-2}\Bigg(|X_{s}|^{2i-j-2}+(2i-j-2)\left(1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)|X_{s}|^{2i-j-3}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=2}^{2i-j-2}{2i-j-2\choose k}\left(1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)^{k}|X_{s}|^{2i-j-2-k}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{i\choose j}2^{j}|z|^{2i-j}L_{3}^{2i-j-2}\Bigg(|X_{s}|^{p-2}+(2i-j-2)\left(1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)|X_{s}|^{p-3}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=2}^{2i-j-2}{2i-j-2\choose k}\left(1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)^{k}|X_{s}|^{p-2-k}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{i\choose j}2^{j}|z|^{2i-j}L_{3}^{2i-j-2}\Bigg(|X_{s}|^{p-2}+\dfrac{(2i-j-2)}{2}\left(|X_{s}|^{p-2}+\left(1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)^{2}|X_{s}|^{p-4}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=2}^{2i-j-2}{2i-j-2\choose k}\left(1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)^{k}|X_{s}|^{p-2-k}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}|X_{s}|^{p-2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{i\choose j}2^{j}|z|^{2i-j}L_{3}^{2i-j-2}\left(i-\dfrac{j}{2}\right)+|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{i\choose j}2^{j}|z|^{2i-j}L_{3}^{2i-j-2}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\left(i-\dfrac{j}{2}-1\right)\left(1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)^{2}|X_{s}|^{p-4}+\sum_{k=2}^{2i-j-2}{2i-j-2\choose k}\left(1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)^{k}|X_{s}|^{p-2-k}\right)

\displaystyle=|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}|X_{s}|^{p-2}\left(\dfrac{i}{L_{3}^{2}}\left(L_{3}^{2}|z|^{2}+2L_{3}|z|\right)^{i}-\dfrac{|z|}{L_{3}}i\left(L_{3}^{2}|z|^{2}+2L_{3}|z|\right)^{i-1}\right)+|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{i\choose j}2^{j}|z|^{2i-j}

\displaystyle\quad\times L_{3}^{2i-j-2}\left(\left(i-\dfrac{j}{2}-1\right)\left(1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)^{2}|X_{s}|^{p-4}+\sum_{k=2}^{2i-j-2}{2i-j-2\choose k}\left(1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)^{k}|X_{s}|^{p-2-k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}|X_{s}|^{p-2}\left(\dfrac{i}{L_{3}^{2}}\left(L_{3}^{2}|z|^{2}+2L_{3}|z|\right)^{i}-\dfrac{|z|}{L_{3}}i\left(L_{3}^{2}|z|^{2}+2L_{3}|z|\right)^{i-1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+L_{3}^{2}\left(1+|X_{s}|+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{i\choose j}2^{j}|z|^{2i-j}L_{3}^{2i-j-2}\Bigg(\big(i-\dfrac{j}{2}-1\big)\big(1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\big)^{2}|X_{s}|^{p-4}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=2}^{2i-j-2}{2i-j-2\choose k}\big(1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\big)^{k}|X_{s}|^{p-2-k}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}|X_{s}|^{p-2}\dfrac{i|z|}{L_{3}}\left(1+L_{3}|z|\right)\left(L_{3}^{2}|z|^{2}+2L_{3}|z|\right)^{i-1}+\sum_{j=0}^{i}|z|^{2i-j}Q_{p}\left(p-2,2i-j,|X_{s}|,1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right),

where
2i-j\geq 2
and

\displaystyle Q_{q}(m,n,x,y)=\sum_{\ell_{1}\leq m,\ell_{2}\leq n,\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=q}c_{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}x^{\ell_{1}}y^{\ell_{2}}.

This, together with the fact that
\sum_{i=2}^{p/2}{p/2\choose i}ia^{i-1}=\frac{p}{2}((1+a)^{p/2-1}-1)
valid for any
a\in\mathbb{R}
, yields to

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{i=2}^{p/2}{p/2\choose i}|X_{s}|^{p-2i}\left(|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z|^{2}+2\left\langle X_{s},c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z\right\rangle\right)^{i}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{|z|}{L_{3}}\left(1+L_{3}|z|\right)|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}|X_{s}|^{p-2}\sum_{i=2}^{p/2}{p/2\choose i}i\left(L_{3}^{2}|z|^{2}+2L_{3}|z|\right)^{i-1}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=2}^{p/2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{p/2\choose i}|z|^{2i-j}Q_{p}\left(p-2,2i-j,|X_{s}|,1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{p}{2}|X_{s}|^{p-2}|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}\dfrac{|z|}{L_{3}}\left(\left(1+L_{3}|z|\right)^{p-1}-L_{3}|z|-1\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=2}^{p/2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{p/2\choose i}|z|^{2i-j}Q_{p}\left(p-2,2i-j,|X_{s}|,1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right).

(8)

As a consequence of (
7
) and (
8
), we have shown that for any
s\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left|X_{s}+c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z\right|^{p}-|X_{s}|^{p}-p|X_{s}|^{p-2}\left\langle X_{s},c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{p}{2}|X_{s}|^{p-2}|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}\left(|z|^{2}+\dfrac{|z|}{L_{3}}\left(\left(1+L_{3}|z|\right)^{p-1}-L_{3}|z|-1\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=2}^{p/2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{p/2\choose i}|z|^{2i-j}Q_{p}\left(p-2,2i-j,|X_{s}|,1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{p}{2L_{3}}|X_{s}|^{p-2}|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}|z|\left(\left(1+L_{3}|z|\right)^{p-1}-1\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=2}^{p/2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{p/2\choose i}|z|^{2i-j}Q_{p}\left(p-2,2i-j,|X_{s}|,1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right).

(9)

Therefore, substituting (
9
) into (
6
), we get that for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda t}|X_{t}|^{p}\leq|x_{0}|^{p}+p\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda s}|X_{s}|^{p-2}\bigg[-\dfrac{\lambda}{p}|X_{s}|^{2}+\langle X_{s},b\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)\rangle+\dfrac{p-1}{2}|\sigma\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\dfrac{1}{2L_{3}}|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|\left(\left(1+L_{3}|z|\right)^{p-1}-1\right)\nu(dz)\bigg]ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}e^{-\lambda s}\sum_{i=2}^{p/2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{p/2\choose i}|z|^{2i-j}Q_{p}\left(p-2,2i-j,|X_{s}|,1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)\nu(dz)ds

\displaystyle\quad+p\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda s}\left|X_{s}\right|^{p-2}\left\langle X_{s},\sigma\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)dW_{s}\right\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}e^{-\lambda s}\left(\left|X_{s-}+c\left(X_{s-},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s-}}\right)z\right|^{p}-\left|X_{s-}\right|^{p}\right)\widetilde{N}(ds,dz).

Now, we define
\tau_{N}:=\inf\{t\geq 0:|X_{t}|\geq N\}
for each
N>0
. Choosing
\lambda=\gamma_{1}p
and using Condition
A7
, Remark
2.2
together with
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}\left(\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}},\delta_{0}\right)=\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\gamma_{1}p(t\wedge\tau_{N})}|X_{t\wedge\tau_{N}}|^{p}\right]\leq|x_{0}|^{p}+{p}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma_{1}ps}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{s}|^{p-2}\right]\left(\gamma_{2}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}\left(\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}},\delta_{0}\right)+\eta\right)ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}e^{-\gamma_{1}ps}\sum_{i=2}^{p/2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{p/2\choose i}|z|^{2i-j}\mathbb{E}\left[Q_{p}\left(p-2,2i-j,|X_{s}|,1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)\right]\nu(dz)ds

\displaystyle\leq|x_{0}|^{p}+{p}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma_{1}ps}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{s}|^{p-2}\right]\left(\gamma_{2}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{s}|^{2}\right]+\eta\right)ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}e^{-\gamma_{1}ps}\sum_{i=2}^{p/2}\sum_{j=0}^{i}{p/2\choose i}|z|^{2i-j}\mathbb{E}\left[Q_{p}\left(p-2,2i-j,|X_{s}|,1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]}\right)\right]\nu(dz)ds.

(10)

Next, using
e^{-\gamma_{1}p(t\wedge\tau_{N})}\geq e^{-\gamma_{1}pt}
and the induction assumption (
5
), there exists a positive constant
C(T,p)
which does not depend on
N
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|X_{t\wedge\tau_{N}}\right|^{p}\right]\leq C(T,p).

(11)

This yields to

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{P}(\tau_{N}<t)\leq\dfrac{C(T,p)}{N^{p}},

which implies that
\tau_{N}
tends to infinity a.s. as
N
tends to infinity. Now, it suffices to let
N\uparrow\infty
and use Fatou’s lemma for the left-hand side of (
11
) to get that
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[|X_{t}|^{p}]\leq C(T,p)
. Thus, by the induction principle, we have shown (
4
).

Step 2:
We next wish to show (
3
) for any even natural number
p\in[2,p_{0}]
.

First, applying (
6
) to
\lambda=2\gamma
and
p=2
, and using
A7
, we get

\displaystyle e^{-2\gamma t}\left|X_{t}\right|^{2}=|x_{0}|^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2\gamma s}\left[-2\gamma\left|X_{s}\right|^{2}+2\left\langle X_{s},b\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)\right\rangle+\left|\sigma\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)\right|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2\gamma s}\left\langle X_{s},\sigma\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)dW_{s}\right\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}e^{-2\gamma s}\left(\left|X_{s-}+c\left(X_{s-},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s-}}\right)z\right|^{2}-\left|X_{s-}\right|^{2}\right)\widetilde{N}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}e^{-2\gamma s}|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)z|^{2}\nu(dz)ds

\displaystyle\leq|x_{0}|^{2}+2\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2\gamma s}\left[-\gamma\left|X_{s}\right|^{2}+\left\langle X_{s},b\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)\right\rangle+\dfrac{1}{2}\left|\sigma\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)\right|^{2}+\dfrac{1}{2}|c\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)\right]ds

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2\gamma s}\left\langle X_{s},\sigma\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)dW_{s}\right\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}e^{-2\gamma s}\left(\left|X_{s-}+c\left(X_{s-},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s-}}\right)z\right|^{2}-\left|X_{s-}\right|^{2}\right)\widetilde{N}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\leq|x_{0}|^{2}+2\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2\gamma s}\left(-\gamma_{2}\left|X_{s}\right|^{2}+\gamma_{2}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}\left(\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}},\delta_{0}\right)+\eta\right)ds+2\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2\gamma s}\langle X_{s},\sigma\left(X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}}\right)dW_{s}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}e^{-2\gamma s}\left(\left|X_{s-}+c\left(X_{s-},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s-}}\right)z\right|^{2}-\left|X_{s-}\right|^{2}\right)\widetilde{N}(ds,dz).

(12)

Thanks to the fact that
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}\left(\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}},\delta_{0}\right)=\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}|^{2}]
, and the estimate (
4
), we get

\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-2\gamma t}|X_{t}|^{2}\right]\leq|x_{0}|^{2}+2\eta\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2\gamma s}ds,

which yields to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{t}|^{2}\right]\leq\begin{cases}\left(|x_{0}|^{2}+\dfrac{\eta}{\gamma}\right)e^{2\gamma t}-\dfrac{\eta}{\gamma}&\text{ if \;\;}\gamma\not=0,\\
|x_{0}|^{2}+2\eta t&\text{ if \;\;}\gamma=0.\end{cases}

Thus, (
3
) holds for
p=2
.

Now, we suppose that (
3
) is valid for all even integer
q\in[2,p-2]
. That is,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{t}|^{q}\right]\leq\begin{cases}C_{q}(1+e^{\gamma qt})&\text{ if \;\;}\gamma\not=0,\\
C_{q}(1+t)^{q/2}&\text{ if \;\;}\gamma=0,q=2\text{\; or \;}\gamma=0,\gamma_{2}>0,q\in(2,p-2],\\
C_{q}(1+t)^{q}&\text{ if \;\;}\gamma=0,\gamma_{2}=0,q\in(2,p-2].\end{cases}\end{split}

(13)

We are going to show that (
3
) holds for even integer
p
. For this, it suffices to use (
10
), the inductive assumption (
13
) and Condition
A5
.

Case
\gamma\neq 0
:
We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\gamma_{1}p(t\wedge\tau_{N})}|X_{t\wedge\tau_{N}}|^{p}\right]\leq|x_{0}|^{p}+C\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma_{1}ps}\left(1+e^{\gamma pt}\right)ds=|x_{0}|^{p}+C\int_{0}^{t}\left(e^{-\gamma_{1}ps}+e^{\gamma_{2}ps}\right)ds.

Thanks to fact that
\tau_{N}\uparrow\infty
a.s. as
N\uparrow\infty
, applying Fatou’s lemma, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\gamma_{1}pt}|X_{t}|^{p}\right]\leq|x_{0}|^{p}+C\int_{0}^{t}\left(e^{-\gamma_{1}ps}+e^{\gamma_{2}ps}\right)ds.

When
\gamma_{1}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{t}|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq|x_{0}|^{p}+C\left(1+t+e^{\gamma_{2}pt}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+e^{\gamma pt}\right).

When
\gamma_{1}\neq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{t}|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq|x_{0}|^{p}e^{\gamma_{1}pt}+\dfrac{C}{p\gamma_{1}}\left(e^{\gamma_{1}pt}-1\right)+\dfrac{C}{p\gamma_{2}}e^{\gamma pt}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+e^{\gamma pt}\right).

Case
\gamma=0
:
When
\gamma_{2}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\gamma_{1}p(t\wedge\tau_{N})}|X_{t\wedge\tau_{N}}|^{p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq|x_{0}|^{p}+C\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma_{1}ps}\left(1+s\right)^{p/2}ds

\displaystyle\leq|x_{0}|^{p}+C\left(1+t\right)^{p/2}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma_{1}ps}ds.

Then, letting
N\uparrow\infty
and using
-\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\gamma_{2}pt}|X_{t}|^{p}\right]\leq|x_{0}|^{p}+C\left(1+t\right)^{p/2}\int_{0}^{t}e^{\gamma_{2}ps}ds\leq|x_{0}|^{p}+\dfrac{C}{\gamma_{2}p}\left(1+t\right)^{p/2}e^{\gamma_{2}pt}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{t}|^{p}\right]\leq C\left(1+t\right)^{p/2}.

When
\gamma_{2}=\gamma_{1}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{t\wedge\tau_{N}}|^{p}\right]\leq|x_{0}|^{p}+C\int_{0}^{t}\left(1+s\right)^{p-1}ds\leq C\left(1+t\right)^{p}.

Then, letting
N\uparrow\infty
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{t}|^{p}\right]\leq C\left(1+t\right)^{p}.

Consequently, (
3
) holds for even integer
p
. Due to the induction principle, (
3
) is valid for any even natural number
p\in[2,p_{0}]
. Finally, (
3
) is also valid for any
p\in[2,p_{0}]
thanks to Hölder’s inequality. This finishes the proof.
∎

3
Propagation of chaos

For
N\in\mathbb{N}
, suppose that
(W^{i},Z^{i})
are independent copies of the couple
(W,Z)
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
. Let
N^{i}(dt,dz)
be the Poisson random measure associated with the jumps of the Lévy process
Z^{i}
with intensity measure
\nu(dz)dt
, and
\widetilde{N}^{i}(dt,dz):=N^{i}(dt,dz)-\nu(dz)dt
be the compensated Poisson random measure associated with
N^{i}(dt,dz)
. Thus, the Lévy-Itô decomposition of
Z^{i}
is given by
Z_{t}^{i}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}z\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz)
for
t\geq 0
. We now consider the system of non-interacting particles which is associated with the Lévy-driven McKean-Vlasov SDE (
1
), where the state
X^{i}=(X_{t}^{i})_{t\geq 0}
of the particle
i
is defined by

\begin{split}X_{t}^{i}&=x_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}b(X_{s}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}})ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(X_{s}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}})dW_{s}^{i}+\int_{0}^{t}c\left(X_{s-}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s-}^{i}}\right)dZ_{s}^{i}\\
&=x_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}b(X_{s}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}})ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(X_{s}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}})dW_{s}^{i}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}c\left(X_{s-}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s-}^{i}}\right)z\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz),\end{split}

(14)

for any
t\geq 0
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.

For
\boldsymbol{x}^{N}:=(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{N}),\boldsymbol{y}^{N}:=(y_{1},y_{2},\ldots,y_{N})\in\mathbb{R}^{dN}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu^{\boldsymbol{x}^{N}},\delta_{0})=\dfrac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|x_{i}\right|^{2}.

Here, the empirical measure is defined by
\mu^{\boldsymbol{x}^{N}}(dx):=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{x_{i}}(dx)
, where
\delta_{x}
denotes the Dirac measure at
x
. Moreover, a standard bound for the Wasserstein distance between two empirical measures
\mu^{\boldsymbol{x}^{N}},\mu^{\boldsymbol{y}^{N}}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu^{\boldsymbol{x}^{N}},\mu^{\boldsymbol{y}^{N}})\leq\dfrac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|x_{i}-y_{i}\right|^{2}=\dfrac{1}{N}\left|\boldsymbol{x}^{N}-\boldsymbol{y}^{N}\right|^{2},

(see (1.24) of
[
4
]
).

Now, the true measure
\mathcal{L}_{X_{t}}
at each time
t
is approximated by the empirical measure

\displaystyle\mu_{t}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}}(dx):=\dfrac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{X_{t}^{i,N}}(dx),

(15)

where
\boldsymbol{X}^{N}=(\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{N})_{t\geq 0}=(X_{t}^{1,N},\ldots,X_{t}^{N,N})_{t\geq 0}^{\mathsf{T}}
, which is called the system of interacting particles, is the solution to the
\mathbb{R}^{dN}
-dimensional Lévy-driven SDE with components
X^{i,N}=(X^{i,N}_{t})_{t\geq 0}

\begin{split}X_{t}^{i,N}&=x_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}b(X_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(X_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})dW_{s}^{i}+\int_{0}^{t}c\left(X_{s-}^{i,N},\mu_{s-}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}}\right)dZ_{s}^{i}\\
&=x_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}b(X_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(X_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})dW_{s}^{i}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}c\left(X_{s-}^{i,N},\mu_{s-}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}}\right)z\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz),\end{split}

(16)

for any
t\geq 0
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.

Observe that the interacting particle system
\boldsymbol{X}^{N}=(X^{i,N})^{\mathsf{T}}_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}
can be viewed as an ordinary Lévy-driven SDE with random coefficients taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{dN}
. Therefore, under Conditions
A1
,
A3
and
A2
valid for
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}=1,L_{1}=L_{2}>0
, there exists a unique càdlàg solution such that

\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{t}^{i,N}|^{2}\right]\leq K,

for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
, where
K>0
does not depend on
N
.

Proposition 3.1
.

Let
X^{i,N}=(X_{t}^{i,N})_{t\geq 0}
be a solution to equation (
16
). Assume Conditions
A6, A7
and that
\sigma
is bounded on
C\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for every compact subset
C
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
A5
holds for
q=2p_{0}
.
Then for any
p\in[2,p_{0}]
, there exists a positive constant
C_{p}
such that for any
t\geq 0
,

\begin{split}\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{t}^{i,N}|^{p}\right]\leq\begin{cases}C_{p}(1+e^{\gamma pt})&\text{ if \;\;}\gamma\not=0,\\
C_{p}(1+t)^{p/2}&\text{ if \;\;}\gamma=0,p=2\text{\; or \;}\gamma=0,\gamma_{2}>0,p\in(2,p_{0}],\\
C_{p}(1+t)^{p}&\text{ if \;\;}\gamma=0,\gamma_{2}=0,p\in(2,p_{0}],\end{cases}\end{split}

where
\gamma=\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}
.

Note that when
\gamma<0
, we have that
\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{t}^{i,N}|^{p}\right]\leq 2C_{p}
.

Proof.

The proof follows the same lines as the one of Proposition
2.5
, thus we omit it.
∎

Next, we provide a result on the propagation of chaos which is the key to the convergence as
N\uparrow\infty
. To simplify the exposition, we define

\displaystyle\varphi(N)=\begin{cases}N^{-1/2}\quad&\textnormal{if\;\;}d<4,\\
N^{-1/2}\ln N\quad&\textnormal{if\;\;}d=4,\\
N^{-2/d}\quad&\textnormal{if\;\;}d>4.\end{cases}

Proposition 3.2
.

Assume that all conditions in Proposition
3.1
hold and that Condition
A2
holds for
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}=1
,
L_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
,
L_{2}\geq 0
. Then, we have

\begin{split}\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|X_{t}^{i}-X_{t}^{i,N}\right|^{2}\right]\leq C_{T}\varphi(N),\end{split}

(17)

for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
, where the positive constant
C_{T}
does not depend on
N
.

Assume further that
L_{1}+L_{2}<0
and
\gamma<0
. Then, we have

\begin{split}\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|X_{t}^{i}-X_{t}^{i,N}\right|^{2}\right]\leq C\varphi(N),\end{split}

(18)

for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
, where the positive constant
C
does not depend on
N
and
T
.

Proof.

Observe that for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle X_{t}^{i}-X_{t}^{i,N}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\left(b(X_{s}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}})-b(X_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})\right)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\sigma(X_{s}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}})-\sigma(X_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})\right)dW_{s}^{i}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}\left(c(X_{s-}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s-}^{i}})-c(X_{s-}^{i,N},\mu_{s-}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})\right)z\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz).

Then, for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, applying Itô’s formula and Condition
A2
valid for
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}=1
,
L_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
,
L_{2}\geq 0
, we obtain that for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda t}|X_{t}^{i}-X_{t}^{i,N}|^{2}=\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda s}\Big(-\lambda|X_{s}^{i}-X_{s}^{i,N}|^{2}+2\langle X_{s}^{i}-X_{s}^{i,N},b(X_{s}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}})-b(X_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+|\sigma(X_{s}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}})-\sigma(X_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})|^{2}\Big)ds+2\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda s}\langle X_{s}^{i}-X_{s}^{i,N},(\sigma(X_{s}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}})-\sigma(X_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}}))dW_{s}^{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}e^{-\lambda s}\left(|X_{s-}^{i}-X_{s-}^{i,N}+\big(c(X_{s-}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s-}^{i}})-c(X_{s-}^{i,N},\mu_{s-}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})\big)z|^{2}-|X_{s-}^{i}-X_{s-}^{i,N}|^{2}\right)\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}e^{-\lambda s}\big|\big(c(X_{s-}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s-}^{i}})-c(X_{s-}^{i,N},\mu_{s-}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})\big)z\big|^{2}\nu(dz)ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda s}\Big(-\lambda|X_{s}^{i}-X_{s}^{i,N}|^{2}+2\langle X_{s}^{i}-X_{s}^{i,N},b(X_{s}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}})-b(X_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+|\sigma(X_{s}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}})-\sigma(X_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})|^{2}+|c(X_{s-}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s-}^{i}})-c(X_{s-}^{i,N},\mu_{s-}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}}|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)\Big)ds

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda s}\langle X_{s}^{i}-X_{s}^{i,N},(\sigma(X_{s}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}})-\sigma(X_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}}))dW_{s}^{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}e^{-\lambda s}\left(\big|X_{s-}^{i}-X_{s-}^{i,N}+\big(c(X_{s-}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s-}^{i}})-c(X_{s-}^{i,N},\mu_{s-}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})\big)z\big|^{2}-|X_{s-}^{i}-X_{s-}^{i,N}|^{2}\right)\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda s}\Big(-\lambda|X_{s}^{i}-X_{s}^{i,N}|^{2}+L_{1}|X_{s}^{i}-X_{s}^{i,N}|^{2}+L_{2}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})\Big)ds

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda s}\langle X_{s}^{i}-X_{s}^{i,N},(\sigma(X_{s}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}})-\sigma(X_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}}))dW_{s}^{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}e^{-\lambda s}\left(\big|X_{s-}^{i}-X_{s-}^{i,N}+\big(c(X_{s-}^{i},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s-}^{i}})-c(X_{s-}^{i,N},\mu_{s-}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})\big)z|^{2}-|X_{s-}^{i}-X_{s-}^{i,N}\big|^{2}\right)\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz).

Therefore, taking the expectation and using the estimate
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i,N}})\leq\mathbb{E}[|X_{s}^{i}-X_{s}^{i,N}|^{2}]
, we obtain that for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|X_{t}^{i}-X_{t}^{i,N}\right|^{2}\right]\leq\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda s}\left((-\lambda+L_{1})\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{s}^{i}-X_{s}^{i,N}|^{2}\right]+L_{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})\right]\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda s}\Bigg((-\lambda+L_{1})\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{s}^{i}-X_{s}^{i,N}|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+L_{2}\bigg(\big(1+\frac{\varepsilon}{L_{2}}\big)\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i}},\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i,N}})\right]+\big(1+\frac{L_{2}}{\varepsilon}\big)\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i,N}},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})\right]\bigg)\Bigg)ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\lambda s}\left(\left(-\lambda+L_{1}+L_{2}+\varepsilon\right)\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{s}^{i}-X_{s}^{i,N}|^{2}\right]+L_{2}\big(1+\frac{L_{2}}{\varepsilon}\big)\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i,N}},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})\right]\right)ds.

(19)

Moreover, from Proposition
3.1
, we have that for any
p\in(4,p_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|X_{t}^{i,N}\right|^{p}\right]\leq C_{T},

for some constant
C_{T}>0
. This, together with
[
5
, Theorem 5.8]
, deduces that

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mathcal{L}_{X_{s}^{i,N}},\mu_{s}^{\boldsymbol{X}^{N}})\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\begin{cases}N^{-1/2}\quad&\textnormal{if\;\;}d<4,\\
N^{-1/2}\ln N\quad&\textnormal{if\;\;}d=4,\\
N^{-2/d}\quad&\textnormal{if\;\;}d>4\end{cases}

\displaystyle=C\varphi(N),

(20)

for any
s\in[0,T]
, where the positive constant
C
does not depend on the time.

Consequently, it suffices to choose
\lambda=L_{1}+L_{2}+\varepsilon
in (
19
) and use the estimate (
3
) to conclude (
17
).

Finally, when
\gamma<0
, it follows from Proposition
3.1
that for any
p\in(4,p_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|X_{t}^{i,N}\right|^{q}\right]\leq C,

where the positive constant
C
does not depend on
T
. Furthermore, when
L_{1}+L_{2}<0
, one can always choose
\varepsilon
sufficiently small such that
\lambda<0
. This allows to conclude (
18
). The desired proof follows.
∎

4
Tamed-adaptive Euler-Maruyama scheme

4.1
Definition

Let
\sigma_{\Delta}=(\sigma_{\Delta,ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq d}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}^{d}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
c_{\Delta}=(c_{\Delta,ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq d}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}^{d}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{d}
be approximations of the coefficients
\sigma
and
c
, respectively, which will be specified later. For all
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
,
\Delta\in(0,1)
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we define the tamed-adaptive Euler-Maruyama discretization of equation (
16
) by

\begin{cases}&t_{0}=0,\quad\widehat{X}_{0}^{i,N}=x_{0},\quad t_{k+1}=t_{k}+\textbf{h}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{t_{k}}^{N},\mu_{t_{k}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}})\Delta,\\
&\widehat{X}_{t_{k+1}}^{i,N}=\widehat{X}_{t_{k}}^{i,N}+b(\widehat{X}_{t_{k}}^{i,N},\mu_{t_{k}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}})(t_{k+1}-t_{k})+\sigma_{\Delta}(\widehat{X}_{t_{k}}^{i,N},\mu_{t_{k}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}})(W_{t_{k+1}}^{i}-W_{t_{k}}^{i})+c_{\Delta}(\widehat{X}_{t_{k}}^{i,N},\mu_{t_{k}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}})(Z_{t_{k+1}}^{i}-Z_{t_{k}}^{i}),\end{cases}

(21)

where

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{t_{k}}^{N}=\left(\widehat{X}^{1,N}_{t_{k}},\ldots,\widehat{X}^{N,N}_{t_{k}}\right),

\displaystyle\mu_{t_{k}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}}(dx):=\dfrac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{\widehat{X}_{t_{k}}^{i,N}}(dx),

\displaystyle\textbf{h}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}_{t_{k}},\mu_{t_{k}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}})=\min\left\{h(\widehat{X}^{1,N}_{t_{k}},\mu_{t_{k}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}}),\ldots,h(\widehat{X}^{N,N}_{t_{k}},\mu_{t_{k}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}})\right\},

and

h(x,\mu)=\dfrac{h_{0}}{(1+|b(x,\mu)|+|\sigma(x,\mu)|+|x|^{l})^{2}+|c(x,\mu)|^{p_{0}}},

(22)

for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and some positive constant
h_{0}
. Here, the constants
\ell
and
p_{0}
are respectively defined in Conditions
A4
and
A7
.

In all what follows, to simplify the exposition, we take
h_{0}=1
in the proofs.

Now, we provide sufficient conditions to ensure
t_{k}\uparrow\infty
as
k\uparrow\infty
, which shows that the tamed-adaptive Euler-Maruyama approximation scheme (
21
) is well-defined.

Proposition 4.1
.

Assume that Condition
A5
holds for
p=2
and there exist positive constants
L,\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{2}
such that the functions
h,b,\sigma_{\Delta}
and
c_{\Delta}
satisfy

T1.

\dfrac{1}{h(x,\mu)}\leq L\left(1+|x|^{\beta_{1}}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\beta_{2}}(\mu,\delta_{0})\right)
and
|b(x,\mu)|\left(1+|b(x,\mu)|\right)h(x,\mu)\leq L
;

T2.

\left\langle x,b(x,\mu)-b(0,\delta_{0})\right\rangle\leq L\left(|x|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})\right)
;

T3.

|\sigma_{\Delta}(x,\mu)|\left(1+|x|\right)\leq\dfrac{L}{\sqrt{\Delta}}
;
|c_{\Delta}(x,\mu)|\left(1+|x|\right)\leq\dfrac{L}{\sqrt{\Delta}}
;
|b(x,\mu)||c_{\Delta}(x,\mu)|\leq\dfrac{L}{\sqrt{\Delta}}
;

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\to+\infty}t_{k}=+\infty\quad\text{a.s.}

Proof.

For all
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
and
H>0
, we define the tamed-adaptive Euler-Maruyama discretization of equation (
16
) as follows

\begin{cases}&t_{0}^{H}=0,\quad\widehat{X}_{0}^{i,N,H}=x_{0},\quad t_{k+1}^{H}=t_{k}^{H}+\textbf{h}^{H}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}_{t_{k}^{H}},\mu_{t_{k}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}})\Delta,\\
&\widehat{X}_{t_{k+1}^{H}}^{i,N,H}=\widehat{X}_{t_{k}^{H}}^{i,N,H}+b_{H}(\widehat{X}_{t_{k}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{t_{k}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}})(t_{k+1}^{H}-t_{k}^{H})+\sigma_{\Delta}(\widehat{X}_{t_{k}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{t_{k}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}})(W_{t_{k+1}^{H}}^{i}-W_{t_{k}^{H}}^{i})\\
&\qquad\quad\quad\quad+c_{\Delta}(\widehat{X}_{t_{k}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{t_{k}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}})(Z_{t_{k+1}^{H}}^{i}-Z_{t_{k}^{H}}^{i}),\end{cases}

(23)

where

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{t_{k}}^{N,H}=\left(\widehat{X}^{1,N,H}_{t_{k}},\ldots,\widehat{X}^{N,N,H}_{t_{k}}\right),

\displaystyle\mu_{t_{k}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}(dx):=\dfrac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{\widehat{X}_{t_{k}}^{i,N,H}}(dx),

\displaystyle\textbf{h}^{H}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}_{t_{k}^{H}},\mu_{t_{k}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}})=\min\left\{h^{H}(\widehat{X}^{1,N,H}_{t_{k}^{H}},\mu_{t_{k}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}),\ldots,h^{H}(\widehat{X}^{N,N,H}_{t_{k}^{H}},\mu_{t_{k}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}})\right\},

\displaystyle h^{H}(x,\mu)=\begin{cases}h(x,\mu)&\text{ if \;\;}|x|^{\beta_{1}}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\beta_{2}}(\mu,\delta_{0})\leq H,\\
\dfrac{1}{1+H}&\text{ if \;\;}|x|^{\beta_{1}}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\beta_{2}}(\mu,\delta_{0})>H,\end{cases}

\displaystyle b_{H}(x,\mu)=\begin{cases}b(x,\mu)&\text{ if \;\;}|x|^{\beta_{1}}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\beta_{2}}(\mu,\delta_{0})\leq H,\\
\dfrac{x}{1+|x|^{2}}+b(0,\delta_{0})&\text{ if \;\;}|x|^{\beta_{1}}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\beta_{2}}(\mu,\delta_{0})>H,\end{cases}

for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then, it can be checked that for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

(1)

|b_{H}(x,\mu)|h^{H}(x,\mu)\leq C_{0}
and
|b_{H}(x,\mu)|^{2}h^{H}(x,\mu)\leq C_{0}
,

(2)

\left\langle x,b_{H}(x,\mu)-b(0,\delta_{0})\right\rangle\leq C_{0}\left(|x|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})\right)
due to Condition
T2
,

for some other positive constant
C_{0}
. Moreover, from Condition
T1
, we have

h^{H}(x,\mu)\Delta\geq\dfrac{\min\{1,L^{-1}\}\Delta}{1+H},

which implies that

t_{k+1}^{H}-t_{k}^{H}\geq\frac{\min\{1,L^{-1}\}\Delta}{1+H}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\to+\infty}t_{k}^{H}=+\infty\quad\text{a.s.}

Now, we define by
\underline{t}^{H}:=\max\left\{t_{k}^{H}:t_{k}^{H}\leq t\right\}
the nearest time point before
t
. The continuous interpolant process is defined by

\begin{split}\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}:&=\widehat{X}_{\underline{t}^{H}}^{i,N,H}+b_{H}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{t}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{t}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)(t-\underline{t}^{H})+\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{t}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{t}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\left(W_{t}^{i}-W_{\underline{t}^{H}}^{i}\right)\\
&\quad+c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{t}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{t}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\left(Z_{t}^{i}-Z_{\underline{t}^{H}}^{i}\right)\\
&=x_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}b_{H}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)dW_{s}^{i}\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)z\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz).\end{split}

(24)

Using Itô’s formula, we get

\displaystyle|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}|^{2}=|x_{0}|^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left(2\left\langle\widehat{X}_{s}^{i,N,H},b_{H}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right\rangle+\left|\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|^{2}\right)ds

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\widehat{X}_{s}^{i,N,H},\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)dW_{s}^{i}\right\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}\left|c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)z\right|^{2}\nu(dz)ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}\left(\left|c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)z\right|^{2}+2\left\langle\widehat{X}_{s-}^{i,N,H},c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)z\right\rangle\right)\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\leq|x_{0}|^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Bigg(2\left\langle\widehat{X}_{s}^{i,N,H}-\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},b_{H}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right\rangle+2\left\langle\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},b_{H}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)-b(0,\delta_{0})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+2\left\langle\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},b(0,\delta_{0})\right\rangle+\left|\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|^{2}+\left|c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)\Bigg)ds

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\widehat{X}_{s}^{i,N,H}-\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)dW_{s}^{i}\right\rangle+2\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)dW_{s}^{i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}\left(\left|c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)z\right|^{2}+2\left\langle\widehat{X}_{s-}^{i,N,H}-\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)z\right\rangle\right)\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}\left\langle\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)z\right\rangle\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\leq|x_{0}|^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Bigg(2\left|\widehat{X}_{s}^{i,N,H}-\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H}\right|\left|b_{H}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|+2C_{0}\left(|\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H}|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0})\right)+|\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H}|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+|b(0,\delta_{0})|^{2}+\dfrac{L^{2}}{\Delta}+\dfrac{L^{2}}{\Delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)\Bigg)ds+2\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\widehat{X}_{s}^{i,N,H}-\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)dW_{s}^{i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)dW_{s}^{i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}\left(\left|c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)z\right|^{2}+2\left\langle\widehat{X}_{s-}^{i,N,H}-\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)z\right\rangle\right)\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}\left\langle\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)z\right\rangle\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz).

(25)

Now, we define
\tau_{R}:=\inf\{t>0:\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}|>R\}
for each
R>0
and
\tau:=s\wedge\tau_{R}
. On the one hand, using equation (
24
), Condition
T3
, the isometry property of stochastic integrals and the fact that
|b_{H}(x,\mu)|h^{H}(x,\mu)\leq C_{0}
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{X}_{\tau}^{i,N,H}-\widehat{X}_{\underline{\tau}^{H}}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}\right]\leq 3\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\left|b_{H}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{\tau}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{\tau}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|^{2}(\tau-\underline{\tau}^{H})^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{\tau}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{\tau}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|^{2}\left|W_{\tau}^{i}-W_{\underline{\tau}^{H}}^{i}\right|^{2}+\left|c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{\tau}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{\tau}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|^{2}\left|Z_{\tau}^{i}-Z_{\underline{\tau}^{H}}^{i}\right|^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 3\left(C_{0}^{2}\Delta^{2}+\dfrac{L^{2}}{\Delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\tau-\underline{\tau}^{H}\right]+\dfrac{L^{2}}{\Delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)\mathbb{E}\left[\tau-\underline{\tau}^{H}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq 3\left(C_{0}^{2}\Delta^{2}+L^{2}+L^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)\right).

(26)

On the other hand, Condition
T3
yields to

\begin{split}&\left|\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|\leq\dfrac{L}{\sqrt{\Delta}},\;\;\left|c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|\leq\dfrac{L}{\sqrt{\Delta}},\\
&\left|\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H}\right|\left|\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|\leq\dfrac{L}{\sqrt{\Delta}},\;\;\left|\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H}\right|\left|c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|\leq\dfrac{L}{\sqrt{\Delta}}.\end{split}

(27)

Therefore, all the stochastic integrals to the Brownian motion and the compensated Poisson random measure above are square martingales. Thus, their moments are equal to zero.

Moreover, using
T3
, equation (
24
), moment properties of the Brownian motion, the isometry property of stochastic integrals, and the fact that
|b_{H}(x,\mu)|^{2}h^{H}(x,\mu)\leq C_{0}
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{X}_{s}^{i,N,H}-\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H}\right|\left|b_{H}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|\big|\mathcal{F}_{\underline{s}^{H}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\left|b_{H}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|^{2}(s-\underline{s}^{H})+\left|b_{H}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|\left|\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|\left|W_{s}^{i}-W_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i}\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|b_{H}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|\left|c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|\left|Z_{s}^{i}-Z_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i}\right|\big|\mathcal{F}_{\underline{s}^{H}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\left|b_{H}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|^{2}(s-\underline{s}^{H})+\dfrac{L}{\sqrt{\Delta}}\left|b_{H}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|\sqrt{s-\underline{s}^{H}}

\displaystyle\quad+\dfrac{L}{\sqrt{\Delta}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)\right)^{1/2}\left|b_{H}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|\sqrt{s-\underline{s}^{H}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\Delta+L\sqrt{C_{0}}+L\sqrt{C_{0}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)\right)^{1/2}.

(28)

This, combined with
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0}){\bf 1}_{s\leq\tau_{R}}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[|\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H}|^{2}{\bf 1}_{s\leq\tau_{R}}\right]
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, yields that for any
t\in(0,T]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{X}_{t\wedge\tau_{R}}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}\right]\leq|x_{0}|^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}C\left(L,\Delta,b(0,\delta_{0}),\mu_{2}\right)\left(1+\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}{\bf 1}_{s\leq\tau_{R}}\right]\right)ds,

where
\mu_{2}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)
.

Next, using equation (
24
) and (
26
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}{\bf 1}_{s\leq\tau_{R}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{X}_{s}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}{\bf 1}_{s\leq\tau_{R}}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{X}_{s}^{i,N,H}-\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{X}_{s\wedge\tau_{R}}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}\right]+6\left(C_{0}^{2}\Delta^{2}+L^{2}+L^{2}\mu_{2}\right).

This implies that for any
t\in(0,T]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{X}_{t\wedge\tau_{R}}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\left(x_{0},L,\Delta,b(0,\delta_{0}),\mu_{2},T\right)\left(1+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{X}_{s\wedge\tau_{R}}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}\right]ds\right),

which, together with Gronwall’s inequality, yields that for any
t\in(0,T]
,

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{X}_{t\wedge\tau_{R}}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}\right]\leq C\left(x_{0},L,\Delta,b(0,\delta_{0}),\mu_{2},T\right).

Then, using Markov’s inequality, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tau_{R}<T)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{X}_{T\wedge\tau_{R}}^{i,N,H}|>R)=N\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{X}_{T\wedge\tau_{R}}^{1,N,H}|>R)

\displaystyle\leq N\dfrac{\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{X}_{T\wedge\tau_{R}}^{1,N,H}|^{2}]}{R^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq N\dfrac{C\left(x_{0},L,\Delta,b(0,\delta_{0}),\mu_{2},T\right)}{R^{2}},

which tends to zero as
R\uparrow\infty
. Therefore,
\tau_{R}\uparrow\infty

as
R\uparrow\infty
. Then due to Fatou’s lemma, we get

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}\right]\leq C\left(x_{0},L,\Delta,b(0,\delta_{0}),\mu_{2},T\right).

(29)

Now, from (
25
), we get that for any
t\in(0,T]
,

\displaystyle|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq|x_{0}|^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\Bigg(2\left|\widehat{X}_{s}^{i,N,H}-\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H}\right|\left|b_{H}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|+2C_{0}\left(|\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H}|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+|\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H}|^{2}+|b(0,\delta_{0})|^{2}+\dfrac{L^{2}}{\Delta}+\dfrac{L^{2}}{\Delta}\mu_{2}\Bigg)ds+2\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\widehat{X}_{s}^{i,N,H}-\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)dW_{s}^{i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)dW_{s}^{i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}\left(\left|c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)z\right|^{2}+2\left\langle\widehat{X}_{s-}^{i,N,H}-\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)z\right\rangle\right)\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz)

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}\left\langle\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)z\right\rangle\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz).

This, combined with (
28
), the fact that
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0})\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[|\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H}|^{2}\right]
and (
29
), deduces that

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}\right]\leq C\left(x_{0},L,\Delta,b(0,\delta_{0}),\mu_{2},T\right)=:\overline{C_{0}}.

(30)

Observe that

\displaystyle\left\{t_{k}\leq T\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{t_{k}\leq T,\;\;\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{\beta_{1}}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\beta_{2}}\left(\mu_{t}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0}\right)\right)\leq\dfrac{H}{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\cup\left\{\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{\beta_{1}}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\beta_{2}}\left(\mu_{t}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0}\right)\right)>\dfrac{H}{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\subset\left\{t_{k}^{H}\leq T\right\}\cup\left\{\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{\beta_{1}}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\beta_{2}}\left(\mu_{t}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0}\right)\right)>\dfrac{H}{2}\right\}.

Then, using
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu_{t}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0})\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}|^{2}\right]
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
and Markov’s inequality, we get that for any
H>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(t_{k}\leq T)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(t_{k}^{H}\leq T\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{\beta_{1}}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\beta_{2}}\left(\mu_{t}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0}\right)\right)>\dfrac{H}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(t_{k}^{H}\leq T\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{\beta_{1}}>\dfrac{H}{4}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\beta_{2}}\left(\mu_{t}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0}\right)>\dfrac{H}{4}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(t_{k}^{H}\leq T\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}>\Big(\dfrac{H}{4}\Big)^{2/\beta_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0}\right)>\Big(\dfrac{H}{4}\Big)^{2/\beta_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(t_{k}^{H}\leq T\right)+\left(\dfrac{4}{H}\right)^{2/\beta_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}\right]+\left(\dfrac{4}{H}\right)^{2/\beta_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu_{t}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(t_{k}^{H}\leq T\right)+\left(\dfrac{4}{H}\right)^{2/\beta_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}\right]+\left(\dfrac{4}{H}\right)^{2/\beta_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(t_{k}^{H}\leq T\right)+\left(\left(\dfrac{4}{H}\right)^{2/\beta_{1}}+\left(\dfrac{4}{H}\right)^{2/\beta_{2}}\right)N\overline{C_{0}}.

Then, let
k\uparrow\infty
and recall that
\lim\limits_{k\to+\infty}t_{k}^{H}=+\infty\quad\text{a.s.}
, we have that for any
H>0
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{P}(t_{k}\leq T)\leq\left(\left(\dfrac{4}{H}\right)^{2/\beta_{1}}+\left(\dfrac{4}{H}\right)^{2/\beta_{2}}\right)N\overline{C_{0}}.

Then, letting
H\uparrow\infty
, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{P}(t_{k}\leq T)=0.

Therefore,
t_{k}\to\infty
in probability as
k\uparrow\infty
. Since
(t_{k})_{k\geq 0}
is an increasing sequence, we have

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\to+\infty}t_{k}=+\infty\quad\text{a.s.}

Thus, the result follows.
∎

Remark 4.2
.

Our approach to showing Proposition
4.1
is quite different from that of
[
10
]
. Indeed, in
[
10
]
, the auxiliary process
\widehat{X}
is constructed by the projection method, which makes its analysis very hard in the case of McKean-Vlasov SDEs. In our proof,
\widehat{X}
is constructed as an Itô process, which allows us to apply Itô’s formula to
|\widehat{X}|^{2}
. This fact helps to greatly simplify our proof.

Let all assumptions of Proposition
4.1
be satisfied, we define by
\underline{t}:=\max\left\{t_{n}:t_{n}\leq t\right\}
the nearest time point before
t
, and by
N_{t}:=\max\left\{n:t_{n}\leq t\right\}
the number of timesteps approximation up to time
t
. Observe that
\underline{t}
is a stopping time. Thus, we define the standard continuous interpolation as

\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N}=\widehat{X}_{\underline{t}}^{i,N}+b\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{t}}^{i,N},\mu_{\underline{t}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}}\right)(t-\underline{t})+\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{t}}^{i,N},\mu_{\underline{t}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}}\right)\left(W_{t}^{i}-W_{\underline{t}}^{i}\right)+c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{t}}^{i,N},\mu_{\underline{t}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}}\right)\left(Z_{t}^{i}-Z_{\underline{t}}^{i}\right).

(31)

Hence,
\widehat{X}^{i,N}=(\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N})_{t\geq 0}
is the solution to the following SDE with jumps

d\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N}=b\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{t}}^{i,N},\mu_{\underline{t}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}}\right)dt+\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{t}}^{i,N},\mu_{\underline{t}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}}\right)dW_{t}^{i}+c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{t}}^{i,N},\mu_{\underline{t}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}}\right)dZ_{t}^{i},\qquad\widehat{X}_{0}^{i,N}=x_{0},

(32)

whose integral equation has the following form

\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N}=x_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}b\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}}^{i,N},\mu_{\underline{s}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}}\right)ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}}^{i,N},\mu_{\underline{s}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}}\right)dW_{s}^{i}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}}^{i,N},\mu_{\underline{s}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N}}\right)z\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz).

In all what follows, the following classical moment estimate for the Brownian motion
W
will be useful

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|W_{t}-W_{\underline{t}^{H}}\right|^{r}\big|\mathcal{F}_{\underline{t}^{H}}\right]\leq C_{r}(t-\underline{t}^{H})^{r/2},

(33)

for any
t>0
,
r>0
and some positive constant
C_{r}
.

4.2
Moments of the tamed-adaptive Euler-Maruyama scheme

We now state the first estimate on the moments of
\widehat{X}^{i,N}=(\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N})_{t\geq 0}
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Assume Conditions
T1
-
T3
and that Condition
A5
holds for
q=2p_{0}
. Then for any
p\in[1,2p_{0}]
and
T>0
, there exists a positive constant

C\left(p,x_{0},L,\Delta,b(0,\delta_{0}),\mu_{2},\mu_{p/2},T\right)
with
\mu_{p/2}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{p/2}\nu(dz)
such that

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N}|^{p}\right]\leq C\left(p,x_{0},L,\Delta,b(0,\delta_{0}),\mu_{2},\mu_{p/2},T\right).

Proof.

Recall that the process
\widehat{X}^{i,N,H}=(\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H})_{t\geq 0}
is defined in (
23
) and (
24
). Using Markov’s inequality, the estimate
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu_{t}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0})\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}|^{2}\right]
and (
30
), we obtain that for any
T>0
,
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
and
H>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N}|\neq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{\beta_{1}}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\beta_{2}}\left(\mu_{t}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0}\right)\right)>H\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{\beta_{1}}>\dfrac{H}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\beta_{2}}\left(\mu_{t}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0}\right)>\dfrac{H}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}>\Big(\dfrac{H}{2}\Big)^{2/\beta_{1}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0}\right)>\Big(\dfrac{H}{2}\Big)^{2/\beta_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\dfrac{2}{H}\right)^{2/\beta_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}\right]+\left(\dfrac{2}{H}\right)^{2/\beta_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu_{t}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\dfrac{2}{H}\right)^{2/\beta_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}\right]+\left(\dfrac{2}{H}\right)^{2/\beta_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\left(\dfrac{2}{H}\right)^{2/\beta_{1}}+\left(\dfrac{2}{H}\right)^{2/\beta_{2}}\right)\overline{C_{0}},

which tends to zero as
H\uparrow\infty
. This implies that
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}|\to\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N}|
in probability as
H\uparrow\infty
. Thus, for any
T>0
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
, there exists a sequence
\{H_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
that tends to infinity such that
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H_{n}}|\to\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N}|
a.s. as
n\uparrow\infty
.

Now, from (
25
), we have that for any
t>0
,
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
and
H>0
,

\displaystyle|\widehat{X}_{t}^{i,N,H}|^{2}\leq|x_{0}|^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\Bigg(2\left|\widehat{X}_{s}^{i,N,H}-\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H}\right|\left|b_{H}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)\right|+2C_{0}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}},\delta_{0})

\displaystyle\quad+|b(0,\delta_{0})|^{2}+\dfrac{L^{2}}{\Delta}+\dfrac{L^{2}}{\Delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}|z|^{2}\nu(dz)\Bigg)ds+2\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\widehat{X}_{s}^{i,N,H}-\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)dW_{s}^{i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\sigma_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)dW_{s}^{i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{0}^{d}}\left(\left|c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)z\right|^{2}+2\left\langle\widehat{X}_{s-}^{i,N,H}-\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},c_{\Delta}\left(\widehat{X}_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{i,N,H},\mu_{\underline{s}^{H}}^{\widehat{\boldsymbol{X}}^{N,H}}\right)z\right\rangle\right)\widetilde{N}^{i}(ds,dz)
